| A.3 | A.4–A.8;A.15;A.37–A.39;A.42;A.44–A.46;C.29–C.40;C.41;C.42;C.43;C.44;A.82;A.83;A.84 | **Refuted** — The prescribed multiplier-one third cusp is false. The canonical GT word satisfies the full pentagon and all maximal pro-p conjugacy tests. Only the global pair ±d can solve its full third cusp. For every peripheral involution the canonical substitution generates in every S4 quotient and is automorphic on the relative pro-two completion over each S3 head. For GT0 involutions it also generates in every individual A5 quotient, satisfies the correctly normalized d-third-cusp identity in every S4 quotient, and is automorphic on the actual joint S4 finite head.  Canonical-head refinement: The first actual relative dyadic relation-module quotient excludes nonidentity fixed heads when the parameter vanishes there. Under the proved self-centralizer hypothesis, one dyadic homology step determines the canonical head from the actual enlarged finite data. This does not prove joint generation or the full third-cusp identity. Regular-pants Alexander refinement: For every regular negative pants involution normalized at z, the unique solution of sigma(g)g^-1=z has a full-unit image in F/<<z>>=Zhat. The substitution x->g,z->z gives an actual conjugacy on F/O^p(<<z>>) for every prime p and on F/R_nil(<<z>>). This conjugacy is in the automorphism group of that relative quotient; original peripheral marking and generation of full F are not asserted. Actual module refinement: A82 proves arbitrary finite-coefficient H1 restriction surjectivity, compact augmentation projectivity at every prime, and actual full-head dyadic freeness of rank two (rank one for the regular pants action). A83 proves odd finite-head twisted duality in G0; A84 upgrades it at every modular odd head and to projective duality over the full completed group ring. The incidence cokernel remains exactly the reduced coset module of the selected generator subgroup. Thus target freeness and twisted duality do not prove marked generation or universal conjugacy. |

B90 proves fixed lifting after the first relative centralization of the actual symmetric intersection, both discretely and in the actual compact quotient. B91 gives the full discrete intersection Alexander module with all transported node coefficients and an exact right-defect Tate criterion over F2[R]. B94 proves independence over the full generic-fiber quotient group ring and determines its involution cohomology. B95 determines the exact intersection with one old-zero translate and its zero excess Tate image. B96 treats every individual old-base translate uniformly: each has source-ideal Tate image, and their individual images sum exactly to J_I. All additional values are isolated in the H2 of the actual orbit-sum kernel. Further checked research proves the two canonical sheet blocks have zero intersection and combine into an F-stable rank128 source block. Sixteen actual row stabilizations refute the sufficient group-unit collision shortcut. An exact G embedding into F13 times F13 proves Z[G] is a domain and yields full independence of each displayed two-column pair, without determining whole-block overlaps. B97 proves the exact rank128 two-sheet block and its full source orbit. B98 proves a domain theorem for the actual integral coefficient ring and refutes the augmented-row-to-group-unit shortcut on complete actual words. B99 determines the entire selected head-kernel/row stabilizer by a two-operator orbit-normal-closure presentation. B100 proves an actual Z3 retract and a nonzero torus attaching sphere in the symmetric surface, refuting its proposed asphericity. Late accepted research, separately retained after the 269-row formal build, determines all eight diagonal stabilizers inside K and identifies the actual E-cover H2 with Z2 and kappa with its Smith edge. B108 proves the actual first-neck slit-model amalgam and its proper full profinite completion, using paired ordinary retractions and cofinal compatible finite edge quotients. B111 proves that restoring the actual q=0 loop kills the nontrivial diagonal [p,bar p], even after full completion. B114 proves actual ordinary annular neck retractions and proper full completion of the abstract bad-vertex amalgam. The actual annular one-neck group is its quotient by two independent based cross commutators, each detected by a prescribed S3 quotient killing the other. The actual source is abstractly free-six and its connected original cover free-eleven; no source injection or centralizer equality for this final quotient is asserted. Late reviewed research after the frozen299-row build proves actual [m,D_s] nontrivial discretely and in full completion, the exact one-sided all-m image, and the strict upper H5 source-kernel bound. A further actual index-two free-seven quotient proves <m,D_s> is discretely free of rank two by its entire integer Schreier orbit. B139 proves the actual ordinary and compact one-family fixed-source equality and exact remaining-kernel reduction. B140 excludes family-kernel repair of either named coarse section in both categories. B141 gives the faithful regional kernel graph and complete mixed square filling, which obstructs the specified regional retractions. Complete late29 research proves arbitrary ordinary/compact coarse nonsplitting, its nonzero actual first-jet class at every odd prime, and no virtual section for the coarse or one-family filling. The exact ordinary fixed source is S amalg_L (L times Z_A), L=H intersect S retained whole. Complete cyclic-leaf filling has a proper ordinary carrier amalgam, but its extra ambient restriction kernel remains Open. B142 proves unrestricted coarse nonsplitting and actual infinite-order/odd-prime extension classes. B143 proves both virtual nonsplitting theorems and the exact zero pullback of these classes to the actual fixed source. B144 gives its complete L=H intersection S presentation. B145/B146 give the whole cyclic normalization and internally proper full leaf carrier. B147 excludes every injective extension of the prescribed center change, even with arbitrary family mixing, by the entire triple link. Late30 determines the ENTIRE ordinary stabilizer L=<m> by the actual invariant pillowcase quotient and faithful whole-source ping-pong. Thus the actual one-family fixed group is Z²*Z and its marked ordinary projection has the explicit section A->A_src,m->m,v->v. The entire original fixed kernel remains unresolved. An exact full first-family free-coset extension retains all opposite-family relations. Latest formal cycle31: B148–B150 give the exact free-three stabilizer normalization, full ordinary cyclic stabilizer, marked section and three-whole-orbit contact-kernel presentation. B151–B152 retain the complete first filling, opposite square base component and mirrored-neck coordinate placement (final survival already B121). B153 proves the actual compact stabilizer directly from the corrected cocycle and two finite-index free bases. B154 proves full induced Ja topology, the marked compact fixed section and the actual source-kernel closure intersection identity. Cycle33: B161-B164 prove the embedded unfilled source double, its full topology, the continuous coset-kernel model and H1 embedding, and the exact remaining contact-image equality with its ordered action and cofinal actual finite-neighborhood criterion. Cycle34: B165 identifies the whole actual deletion-kernel H1 with the completed source coset space and gives a continuous pointed section from filling cohomology. B166 identifies the whole filling H1 with a left-coset space of the actual fixed subgroup and the contact source with its exact closed subspace. Cycle35: the entire compact single-contact m-orbit kernel is a continuous free profinite family. Cycle36: the entire one-sided contact image has full induced topology and a literal continuous section. Its exact normal defect survives in a complementary retained target, also fully determined. Mirrored deletions have equal restrictions. The defect has a retained image containing the entire derived free-two subgroup. |

## What was read

The component source logs are `work_A/A_report.md`, `work_CE/source_log.md`, and `work_DE/source_log_DE.md`. Root's sources include the primary published Boggi and Boggi–Zalesskii texts, Harbater–Schneps, Lochak–Schneps, Nakamura–Schneps, Hoshi–Mochizuki–Tsujimura, Deligne–Mostow, Thurston, Allcock, Asada, Goncalves–Guaschi, Kirby–Scharlemann, and the Platonov–Rapinchuk strong-approximation theorem. Precise theorem references and applicability are given in the paper. Newly read bibliography records are marked verified. No claim of originality is made. The appendix source log is being consolidated as reading proceeds.

## Computations and their limits

All 70 entries of the supplied manifest passed byte-count and SHA-256 checks. Exact programs verify the class-three order16807 counterexample, the modulo12 framed obstruction, the standard-extension finite-subgroup obstruction, the torus section-generator example, and the nonabelian cohomology toy models. For the icosahedron we give exact Artin automorphism identities, all finite triangle and binary-icosahedral coset tables, 495 cross-ratio pairs over Q(zeta20), a length384 common-kernel word detected in SL2(F101), a weight-four special-derivation counterexample, and the Eisenstein eigenspace matrices. Five-point automorphism, subgroup and decorated census calculations are included with their outputs. Floating geometric braid discovery is identified as discovery only; the resulting braid identities are checked independently by exact group calculations.

## Corrections and unresolved claims

The specified conjugator in task A.3 need not preserve the third cusp; this does not refute conjugacy of the original involution. R2 does not preserve finite order without an additional equivariant obstruction argument. H1(C2,H) already has continuum many classes of full-standard conjugates, so its size does not count exotic full conjugacy classes. R3 remains a valid parity obstruction, but no parity-one full involution is constructed. The R5 closure-intersection step remains unsupported in the nonsolvable case; the corresponding published theorem is not refuted. In P3 and P4, maps require the stated stabilizer domains, and forgetting the order-two factor gives C3*C3. P7's graded assertion is false, with an exact weight-four certificate. B.57 now independently refutes its full-group equality by an actual central-extension lift; that conclusion is not inferred from the graded calculation. Lead7 requires semilinearity and deck-choice corrections. The original manuscript admits anti-automorphisms of orders6 and10; on scan33 the word after14 is struck out, and the correct count is14 families and16 orbits. Full details and limits of the independent torus and real-structure audits are in the component reports.

Every undecided requested line appears in the outcome table and in `task_coverage.csv`. The component reports retain attempted approaches, exact missing implications, and the next research directions. This checkpoint records progress without claiming completion.

## Second-hour advances and remaining main obstructions

The current table supersedes the first checkpoint's provisional table. In particular, the first checkpoint's description of P4 as having an unproved domain is superseded by B.53. Its first two resolvents are now constructed on the exact quadratic-character domain, and the third filling is given its correct weighted-orbifold target. The first checkpoint's P6 task label was too broad: B.20 proves the good-prime fixed-group and peripheral-lift statements, but its own proof does not justify the additional unrestricted conclusion about images of the full centralizer. The task table now marks that remaining implication Open. No mathematical proof of that implication was removed.

### Main question A

A.23–A.24 prove relative torsion and cusp centralizer statements and show that the parity character factors through the actual outer image at U12. A.25 then constructs an actual involution there with multiplier minus one and parity one. A.26 detects the action and frame parity in the finite quotient of order 576·6^97 and rules out continuation by full Galois elements. A.29 shows that this relative involution has exactly three section classes, so the section-count obstruction does not eliminate it. A.30 strengthens the input further: the actual relative normalizers of the two torsion factors can be made genuine S3 and V4 subgroups, preserving the required frame values. An R2 lift's square then lies in the kernel retaining both the actual relative action and the full relative frames.

The remaining main step is an exact norm equation in that framed kernel, or in the larger kernel when the relative frames are allowed to vary. It is neither solved nor shown insoluble. The relative perfectness used in R2 is a statement about the residual subgroup of the free group; it is not a proof that this automorphism kernel has no abelian 2-primary quotient. A.27 records the finite real-reflection count with its necessary incidence data. A.28 gives a checked finite A6 action which is obstructed already by a central double cover; it is not valid input to R2 and is not presented as a counterexample to a lifting theorem.

### Main question B

B.29–B.34 establish the actual arithmetic setting of Lead8: the tetrahedral centralizer has its full induced profinite topology, its configuration curve and icosahedral point are explicitly defined over Q(sqrt5), and the whole Galois group of that field normalizes K. Every conjugate cusp inertia meets K trivially. The cyclic-normalizer argument proves that abelian K would be trivial, including the precise cofinite-support assertion and its totally real strengthening. B.50–B.51 push this beyond abelianity: every nontrivial Sylow subgroup would be a nonabelian free pro-p group; K has trivial center, no nontrivial Galois-stable pro-odd subgroup, and no finite derived length. Complex conjugation has trivial fixed subgroup in the ambient tetrahedral free profinite group. These restrictions do not establish K=1.

B.46–B.49 identify the exact finite-index product-separability condition and explain why individual subgroup separability or a single closed product is insufficient. The obstruction is illustrated by a fully checked congruence-kernel example, including a version with both factors torsion-free free groups. B.52 proves that the actual unrefined pure tetrahedral–cyclic product is a finite word-equation locus and hence is closed. The proof classifies the four pure conjugacy classes of labelled A5 embeddings by a braid rotation residue. Finite unions give closure for the full centralizer product and for an explicitly normalized rank-two cyclic factor containing its pure subgroup. Arbitrary finite-index refinements remain equivalent to the unresolved K=1 assertion. Thus the product route requested in B.4(c) is established in its stated geometric normalization, but the main centralizer decision does not follow from that unrefined result.

B.53 constructs both quintic resolvent homomorphisms on ker chi_-15, with their multipliers, their exact arithmetic comparison under geometric markings over Q(sqrt(-15)), and K in both kernels. The third filling has target the peripheral outer group of C3*C3. The finite sign computation and affine markings have an exact executable certificate. At the second checkpoint, neither the nonidentity witness requested in P5 nor the asserted identity on every GT element had been proved. B.55 now proves the first assertion; the full-GT identity remains open, as detailed in the third-hour update below.

B.42–B.45 show why the two cited relative lifting prescriptions cannot be assigned unique tripod or resolvent values. A nontrivial common Galois kernel preserves all the prescribed relative actions, both frames, and the multiplier. Multiplying any allowed lift by this kernel changes all tripod and all three corrected resolvent values injectively. The proof gives an explicit S10000 Galois quotient and a uniform finite simple-section exclusion for the relevant linear and pro-p representations. This proves nonuniqueness of the prescriptions, not existence of any allowed lift inside K. At most one lift in each such arithmetic coset can belong to K.

The published-centralizer dependency audit is complete in B.36 and records/centralizer_dependency_audit.md. No proved statement in the supplied returns was found whose proof requires the disputed nonsolvable equality. The separately retrieved ANABELIAN149 A.29l uses the independent 2019 orbifold lemma in its stated hypotheses. The published nonsolvable centralizer assertions themselves remain undecided.

D.17 constructs parameter-relative equivariant nullhomotopies through continuous maps with the actual uniform-tail control. It does not upgrade those maps to homeomorphisms; that remains the missing implication for noncompact operator quotients. C.27 excludes every mixed-sign diagonal power involution from the full torus peripheral group by finite mixed-prime quotients, although each pro-p image is a standard reflection. It settles that explicit family only. The scalar-cubic power construction also fails an exact class-four cusp test; an IA correction is not excluded. ## Third-hour advances: full resolvent witnesses and the congruence involution tower

The present table supersedes the second checkpoint wherever a status or obstruction has changed. The two main decisions remain open, but the advances below address explicit requested assertions by full group constructions. They are not additional finite samples of an unchanged open implication.

### Full nonidentity witnesses for P5 and a strict common-kernel result for P7

B.54 begins with an actual Galois automorphism of the three-cusp group having a nonzero degree-three class modulo7, certified by a cyclotomic-unit calculation. After a boundary normalization, it inserts that automorphism into a disc containing two cusps of an arbitrary torsion-free finite-index modular cover. The insertion is an actual automorphism, including when the complementary surface has handles. Coprime fixed-point lifting in the closed pro-seven automorphism group realizes the entire finite trace, and the peripheral conjugators are then glued over all relative primes before R2. This avoids any claim that an arbitrary Lie derivation integrates. For the icosahedral cover its tripod trace is nonconstant:160 tripods give zero and60 give10 times the nonzero arithmetic class. Consequently every full lift of these specified relative data lies outside the braid image and outside the Galois image. The universal assertion that R2 can always be performed inside the braid group is false.

B.55 uses the same actual insertion at a finite quotient of order29160 that simultaneously controls the original and both quintic level-two targets. Its two induced thirty-letter representations have the same kernel. Exact enumeration also verifies the filled-image equalities, all2916 cusp classes, and the incidence count at every individual cusp. The original outer degree-three action is zero, while both resolvent actions are30 times the nonzero class modulo7. Thus both quintic homomorphisms are nonidentity on their domain, already at multiplier one, and every member of the specified nonempty full R2 lift set is a witness. Independent review prompted one precision improvement: integer incidence constancy is now checked directly, rather than inferred from linear independence over F7. The witnesses are not asserted to satisfy the pentagon. The second assertion of P5, the identity on every GT element in the domain, remains open.

B.57 refutes the full common-kernel equality of P7 by a different construction. It retains the exact quotient by the intersection of all fifteen conjugated filling kernels, so the three target identities are imposed on the full groups. A specified seven-cycle pair generates A9 on a tripod. Its sixty conjugated kernels have distinct cusp supports; iterated commutators prove the full regular perfect base A9^60, not just a subdirect approximation. Each filling kernel still maps onto that base, giving the exact infinite joint quotient. Diagonal conjugation by an odd transposition fixes the center of the universal cover (2.A9)^60. This supplies an automorphism of the actual central refinement of the source quotient, preserving the actual s, r and cusp conjugacy classes there. The closed-normal central-quotient criterion produces full peripheral lifts.

Every such lift has all three corrected resolvent values exactly one, but an outer A9 action detects a nontrivial tripod. It therefore lies outside K. The common kernel strictly contains K and contains a closed copy of Z2. This does not construct a nontrivial centralizing mapping class and does not decide K=1. B.17's formerly Open full equality is accordingly relabelled Refuted, with the independent proof in B.57; the graded proof B.16 is neither altered nor promoted to an integration theorem. Root and the PartA agent independently checked the complete proof; the PartD/E agent independently checked the finite certificate and all fifteen filling/cusp comparisons. Their review notes are retained.

### The involution tower and the exact surviving obstruction

A.31 chooses an inertia-at-three Galois cocycle whose complete Kummer character of two vanishes while both normalized CM bits are nonzero. Its dyadic relative action glues with the identity at every odd prime over every principal-congruence base. Compactness of the nonempty full lift sets yields one full automorphism with multiplier minus one and parity one whose images are involutions throughout that entire relative tower. A.33 also retains equivariant literal frames, so its square lies in the common framed kernel. A.32 proves that no c-invariant fourth-root character of parity can depend solely on that tower or its CM refinements, including moduli24 and48.

This is not a full involution. The relative principal-congruence tower is proved noncofinal: its common group kernel maps onto A7, whereas all its finite quotient composition factors come from PSL2 over prime fields and cyclic groups. The norm equation in the omitted automorphism kernel remains unsolved. A.34 extends the peripheral lifting criterion to every closed normal relative subgroup, and identifies the tower kernel as the pronilpotent residual of the congruence kernel; it does not preserve finite order. All these limitations are part of the statements themselves.

### The complete proposed test family in B.4(a) has a nontrivial kernel

B.56 gives an explicit nontrivial element h of the discrete pure tetrahedral centralizer killed by every rigid five-, six- and seven-point map. Its geometric monodromy is certified by rational Sturm isolation on every path segment and an exact Artin action calculation, rather than a floating braid approximation. The intrinsic tripod actions and the three filled tetrahedral-orbifold actions are identically trivial on that pure centralizer and its completion. Those are carefully typed intrinsic maps; a general tetrahedral-centralizing element is not asserted to extend to the full modular group. Thus h lies in the full test intersection proposed in B.4(a). A commutator with the earlier four-point kernel word also kills all495 four-point maps and is proved nontrivial. Residual finiteness retains these nontrivial elements in the completion. The whole test kernel is not classified. These explicit discrete false positives are outside K, so they eliminate the proposed faithfulness route without deciding the original centralizer question.


### Further checked consequences before the third checkpoint

A.35 applies B.57 while retaining the whole congruence relative quotient and both frames. It proves that the exact correction kernel L_cong^fr contains a closed Z2, also in all three resolvent kernels. This excludes a proposed coprime splitting argument based on that kernel being pro-odd. It does not give the action of the chosen negative lift on this subgroup or the class of its square, and therefore is not an obstruction calculation for the actual norm equation.

The B.56 kernel computation is strengthened by a precise primary theorem of Shusterman (2016), Theorem1.2 and Corollary3.7 in the opened primary preprint. The entire intrinsic B.4(a) test intersection equals the kernel of one specified seven-point map. Its discrete kernel and the intersection obtained by adjoining all495 four-point maps are free groups of countably infinite rank. Each closed completed-test kernel is an infinite-index normal subgroup of the finite-rank free profinite ambient group and contains an explicit nontrivial finite word, h or k. The finite-word theorem therefore makes each free profinite of countably infinite rank. Normality and infinite index alone are not being used as a freeness criterion. The full completions of the discrete kernels are nonmetrizable, while the completed-test kernels are metrizable; they are consequently different objects. Equality with the embedded closure of the discrete kernel remains unproved. The remaining explicit embedded-kernel computation is the full list of thirteen meridian images.

C.28 proves that every section of a full pure negative involution on any punctured sphere has trivial geometric centralizer. The proof uses the finite Boolean localized cohomology algebra to count exactly n+1 section classes, an exact peripheral-incidence cycle to place each class at two endpoints, and arbitrary finite permutation coefficients in surface-pair duality to kill the full centralizer, including its possible odd-primary part. It then proves fixed-point-freeness through actual compatible genus-zero Birman towers. No free-product recognition of the extension and no five-point action of GT0 is assumed.


### Conjugator descent and the pentagon reduction

A.36 proves a precise new reduction of the main involution question. If an involution alpha already belongs to GT and alpha=g c g^(-1) for some g in the full peripheral group P, that same g belongs to GT. A.6 first supplies GT0 membership. In the actual parenthesized-braid groupoid, the normalized intertwining equation makes the arrow A h fixed by alpha. The five literal inserted arrows have an alpha-fixed based pentagon discrepancy in the full pure braid group on four strands. C.28 makes that fixed subgroup trivial. This proves the pentagon for h without presuming that g already acts on the five-point groupoid. The two hexagons come from its established GT0 membership; a closed-submonoid argument checks the unit condition. Root and the CE agent independently reviewed the proof and the primary presentation in Boavida de Brito, Horel, and Robertson (2019), Lemma7.1 and Proposition7.3.

A.37 removes even the assumption that a peripheral conjugator exists from the pentagon part of the argument. For a GT involution, the inverse u of the unique R1 coboundary is itself a full profinite pentagon solution. Conjugacy is equivalent to two remaining conditions: the images x and u^(-1) y u generate the full profinite free group, and some unit multiplier makes the third cusp correct. The first condition is independent of the unit. Furusho (2010), Section3, Corollaries12–13 and Question14, was opened at the exact boundary: its pro-prime conclusions can allow a quadratic extension of the multiplier, and the full profinite implication is stated separately as a question there. No integral unit, full hexagon, or full generation assertion is imported from a prounipotent theorem. These statements concern involutions already in GT; they do not make all three universal conjugacy questions equivalent or decide any of them.


### Late reviewed advance: unit peripheral powers force surjectivity

Immediately after the formal-source freeze, an independent covering-space proof recovered the automatic-generation criterion already asserted in the supplied LM4 D.35 and generalized it to bordered oriented surfaces. It removes the generation condition from A.37 once its unit third-cusp condition holds; this recovery is an audit of that prior row, not a new resolution of the main question. For a continuous endomorphism of the full profinite fundamental group of a compact oriented surface with boundary, assume each individual peripheral is sent to a conjugate of a unit power of a distinct peripheral. If the compact image were proper, place it in a proper open subgroup. The corresponding finite surface cover would contain one width-one boundary over each base boundary. Passing the surface relation to the cover's completed abelianization gives a relation, with unit coefficients, supported on those boundaries. The only boundary relation is the sum of all oriented boundaries. Therefore the selected boundaries are all the boundaries of the cover, their coefficients agree, and every base boundary has exactly one lift of width one. The cover has degree one, a contradiction. Surjectivity and the Hopf property give an automorphism. Profinite conjugators are reduced to discrete coset representatives before applying this boundary calculation.

Root and the PartA and PartC agents checked the pants specialization independently. The complete proposed general proof is retained as work_CE/research_scratch_C29.txt; formal insertion and registration are scheduled immediately after the third snapshot. Thus A.37 remains correct as printed, but its two-condition formulation has a redundancy: the remaining issue is the existence of one full profinite unit multiplier satisfying the third-cusp conjugacy equation. Neither that existence nor the full involution question is decided. Prime-by-prime tests alone do not supply the full equation.

## Fourth-hour mathematical advances and supersessions

The preceding checkpoint narratives are preserved as historical records. The statements in this section supersede their descriptions of unfinished generator lists, the redundant generation condition, and the local square-root obstruction. None of the quotient involutions below is asserted to be a full involution, and none of the linear detection results proves that the actual residual centralizer is trivial.

### A single full equation remains for a prescribed involution

C.29 formalizes the independently reviewed covering proof of peripheral co-Hopfianity for full profinite fundamental groups of compact oriented surfaces with boundary. A continuous endomorphism sending the distinct named boundaries to conjugates of unit powers of distinct boundaries is an automorphism. A proper compact image would lie in a proper open subgroup. In the corresponding finite surface cover, unit powers supply width-one lifts of every selected boundary. The image surface relation, in completed integral homology, is supported on these lifts. The unique relation among all oriented boundary classes forces them to be all the boundaries of the cover. The covering degree is then one. The argument includes the single-boundary case and keeps the full induced topology. This independently verifies and generalizes the pants automatic-generation assertion already present in supplied LM4 D.35; no priority is claimed.

A.39 consequently expresses full conjugacy of a given involution alpha in the peripheral group as a single condition on its unique R1 parameter u: there must exist a full unit mu such that x^mu u^(-1)y^mu u is conjugate to (xy)^mu in the full free profinite group. Full generation follows from that equation. If any such multiplier exists, exactly two do, differing by the global sign; this uses the already proved centralizer of standard complex conjugation. Existence is detected by the full cofinal system of finite characteristic quotients and compactness. It is not detected by the maximal pro-prime quotients alone. For an involution already in GT, A.36 still puts any existing peripheral conjugator in GT, and A.37 still supplies the full pentagon for u.

C.30 derives the third-cusp conjugacy directly from the two hexagon/duality equations before applying C.29. Thus a unit pair satisfying these equations automatically gives a full free-group automorphism. With the pentagon, compact closed-submonoid inversion gives an actual GT element. The independent finite comparison `check_unconstrained_substitution.py` shows why the equations matter: a unit pair with f=[x,y]^2 has image of order12 in a displayed A5 quotient whose original generators have image of order60. Arbitrary unit substitutions are not asserted to be automorphisms.

### Integral local pentagons and the remaining mixed-prime problem

C.31 proves that the full profinite parenthesized-braid endomorphism monoid itself has only unit multipliers. The argument constructs actual full braid endomorphisms by quotienting the full labelled subgroupoid on a free S_n orbit. This retains the permutations; an individual object's automorphism group would only give pure braids. The profinite ribbon-extension theorem, checked directly in Boavida de Brito–Horel–Robertson, gives the exact full-twist identity phi_n(z_n)=z_n^lambda without assuming surjectivity. Goodness and actual finite cohomological dimension make the full profinite braid group torsion-free. If p divided lambda, the image of the p-strand rotation, corrected by a central power of z_p, would have order p and nontrivial p-cycle permutation. Therefore lambda is a full unit. Together with C.30, the full monoid is its group of units. Searches and the exact primary-source comparisons are retained; no claim of literature priority is made.

C.32 proves the corresponding statement for the **relative** braid completion which retains S_n and replaces the pure braid subgroup by its pro-p completion. The full induced pro-p topology is proved from the almost-direct free-group structure. A finite p-extension comparison then identifies the preimage of a Sylow p subgroup of S_n with the full pro-p completion of its discrete preimage. Its p-goodness and actual finite cohomological dimension exclude p-torsion. The same ribbon/rotation argument excludes every p-divisible integral multiplier. This statement is not about the maximal pro-p quotient of the ordinary braid group, and the proof uses no virtual-dimension shortcut.

C.33 supplies the remaining integral step for a pro-p pentagon word f. Furusho's theorem supplies a prounipotent hexagon with a possibly quadratic root mu of D=1+24c2(f). The integral affine Magnus coefficient F(a,b), constructed from the commutator word, is symmetric by duality. Evaluating the hexagon at x=(a,T), y=(a^(-1),0), a=1+T, and adding the inverse specialization gives, for nonzero mu,

    (a^mu-1)(1-T^2 F(a,a^(-1))/a)=mu T a^((mu-1)/2).

The parenthesized factor has integral coefficients. Comparing the first potentially nonintegral coefficient of (a^mu-1)/(mu T), at degree p-1, forces the product of mu-j for j=1,...,p-1 to be divisible by p. Hence D^((p-1)/2)=1 modulo p, so D is a nonzero square and Hensel's lemma supplies a unit root in Z_p. No integrality of a general quadratic binomial series beyond the degrees actually used is assumed. The zero case is separately excluded by C.32; no limit with f held fixed is taken. At two and three the elementary congruences of D already give square units. Root and the A component independently checked every affine product and coefficient comparison. An additional exact symbolic program and its actual output are attached.

A.42 applies this to the canonical word of every full GT involution. Its prescribed peripheral conjugator now exists on **every maximal pro-p quotient**, and D is a square unit in the full ring Zhat. This supersedes A.38's unresolved local residue condition while retaining A.38 as the exact criterion from which the improvement follows. It does not treat all relative characteristic levels or produce a full hexagon.

C.34 makes the sign problem explicit. Let f=1, let mu_2=-1 and let mu_p=1 at every odd prime. Then mu^2=1 and the full pentagon holds, but mu=7 modulo24 and m=(mu-1)/2=3 modulo12. In S4, with x=(234), y=(1234), and z=(xy)^(-1), the full hexagon has image x^m z^m y^m=x, not the identity. The source's literal assertion for a **specified** square root therefore fails. This is expressly distinguished from the existential interpretation of Furusho's Question14: in this example the global roots 1 and -1 work. The existence of a suitable root for the canonical word in the full profinite group remains the main unresolved step.

The first mixed finite-base exploration is also retained, without promotion to a full counterexample. Exact S3 pentagon tests on all1,008 homomorphisms from the four-strand pure braid group leave a sign-character commutator coordinate unconstrained. One value of that coordinate prevents every unit hexagon residue in S3. These are compatible **finite test data**, not an actual full pentagon word or a full involution. The next comparison must retain the finite quadratic character, which ordinary prounipotent completion loses.

### The parity-one tower passes noncongruence and central layers

A.40 adds an actual noncongruence quotient to the previously constructed compatible parity-one involution tower. The proof then uses the **actual** central refinement required by the lifting criterion. Relative perfectness R=[R,Pi] forces the full preimage of the finite perfect base to be the finite image of its universal central cover. Genuine order-two and order-three source elements construct a section over the retained quotient. Explicit centralizer surjections retain the old literal frames. Ordinary peripheral lifting then produces a full negative element of parity one whose action on this enlarged quotient has order two. Its square in the full group remains uncontrolled.

A.41 applies the same construction to finite unions of all the specified noncongruence simple-tripod families. A.43 further proves that the first relative central refinement is enough for each such finite family. If W is the retained kernel, then [[W,Pi],Pi]=[W,Pi]. ### A genuine relative mixed-base advance

A.44 excludes the bad dihedral coordinate for the canonical parameter of **every full peripheral involution**, without assuming the pentagon. In a quotient D_(2p) with x and y reflections, the induced involution is either the identity or the reflection inner action. The latter would force the two global section classes at the third cusp to have the same central image, contradicting the nontrivial image of that cusp. In the identity case, a hypothetical failure of the canonical substitution to generate the dihedral group yields a degree-p pants cover with p+2 cusps and genus zero. All its cusp classes are fixed and inverted. Its exact section-incidence graph would be one triangle and (p-1)/2 separate digons. C.28 proves that such a pure negative sphere action instead has one cycle through all cusps, a contradiction.

The proof retains every cusp frame. In particular it does not make the invalid inference that a trivial finite quotient action makes every cusp in an arbitrary cover individually fixed. In the degree-p reflection-stabilizer cover the third cusp is unique, which supplies that missing comparison. The degree-three case lists all five representatives and frames explicitly. Odd-prime detection, together with the two-primary parity, gives surjectivity on every finite dihedral reflection quotient.

Let V be the index-two subgroup defined by sending both x and y to the nontrivial element of C2. Its free rank-three homology splits at every odd prime into a two-dimensional invariant plane and a one-dimensional sign line. The canonical substitution fixes the invariant coordinates; its surjectivity on the dihedral quotient detects the sign coordinate. Frattini then gives a genuine automorphism of F/O^p(V). At p=2 this relative quotient is the maximal pro-two quotient already covered by R1. The full intertwining equation descends, so these are unmarked automorphism conjugators at this entire specified family of relative levels. They are not asserted to preserve the third cusp with multiplier one or to cover every finite base.

### Nonabelian centralizer refinements with one line left over

B.59 carries the rigid-seven computation to the actual degree-two quotient gamma2(P)/gamma3(P), an integral lattice of rank165. The combined target matrix is240 by165 and has rank159. The actual A5 action on this kernel is then computed from normalized pair-linking coordinates and the integral infinitesimal braid brackets. All165 coefficients, including45 nonzero coefficients, and the three explicit6-by6 generator matrices are attached. Since K already has zero ambient degree-one image, kills the rigid targets, and is fixed by A5, its image in the full completion of P/gamma3(P) lies on Zhat w. Full profinite topology is justified using integral class-two normal-form coordinates and cofinal finite coordinate quotients. If c is the displayed commutator product representing w and R is generated by gamma3(P) and c, then K lies in the closure of R. The quotient P/R is torsion-free nilpotent of class two, with abelian rank54 and commutator rank164. Every finite quotient factoring through it therefore gives valid both-factor refinements of the centralizer product. These include genuinely nonabelian finite quotients.

The surviving central line is a possible quotient image, not a constructed centralizing mapping class. universal full involution-conjugacy questions and the actual icosahedral
centralizer decision remain Open.

## Sixth-hour advances and exact remaining obstructions

The formal sixth-hour snapshot adds twelve proved statements,
A.50–A.54, B.62–B.64 and C.37–C.40. The two main decisions remain
Open. The new results address the scalar and marked-cusp obstructions
in PartA and the actual nonnilpotent representation in PartB; they do
not replace either full question by its quotient version.

### A uniform scalar bound, and its precise next-base limitation

A.53 proves a general theorem for every finite abelian residual layer
over an actual relative pronilpotent base. Let R=R_nil(K0), C=Pi/R,
and V=R/W be finite abelian, with W closed normal in Pi. Define
W0=W and W(i+1)=[Wi,Pi], with closed commutators. If the dyadic and
triadic primary exponents of V are2^e2 and3^e3, absent primary parts
having exponent parameter zero, then

    W(m+1)=Wm,  where m=max(1,e2+1,2e3+1).

For an elementary layer the depths are two at prime2, three at prime3,
and one at every prime at least5. These are actual scalar subgroups,
not a count of possible extension modules. At every stage the residual
preimage has abelianization V and nilpotency class at most two; its
derived center is a finite quotient of the integral exterior square
of V. The finite action on each primary center factors through the
corresponding maximal prime-power quotient of C2*C3. Exact augmentation
identities give the bound.

The proof uses cohomology of the actual retained infinite group. Its
normal pronilpotent subgroup splits into characteristic Sylow factors;
relative perfectness and finite prime-group Nakayama remove the
prime-to-p invariants. Averaging on that actual normal subgroup proves
the required H1 vanishing. The conditional version also applies over
an enlarged base if that cohomology condition is independently checked.

A.54 determines exactly why this qualification matters. After adjoining
the stabilized layer, write C'=Pi/Wm and P=R/Wm. For the stated
acyclic coefficient modules T, restriction gives

    H1(C',T)=Hom_C(V,T).

In particular H1(C',V)=End_C(V) is nonzero for V nonzero. The proof
uses the continuous Hochschild–Serre and inflation–restriction
sequences, checked in Poonen (2017, Theorem6.7.3 and Corollary6.7.4,
p.194). An explicit second filiform cocycle, with exact ranks21,22,23,
produces a legitimate next residual layer for which the required
dual-coefficient H1 is nonzero. Thus automatic acyclicity does fail
under successive reuse of a module. Simultaneous enlargement over
the original base remains covered by A.53. Neither conclusion is a
negative norm obstruction.

A.50 supplies the actual Heisenberg frame layer with nonzero torsion
cohomology. Its uncorrected central cover fails the source order-two
relation; a specified C12 carry repairs that relation, and the retained
frame norms are checked exactly. A.51 gives a concrete strict second
scalar refinement: W2 is properly contained in W1, with quotient C2,
and W3=W2. A.52 pairs the construction with its actual c-pullback.
Two literal commutator words separate the module supports, and the
paired center increments have dimensions two and four. The construction
retains the earlier frames and quotient actions. It still supplies
only a noncofinal compatible family of involutory images of a full
negative element, whose full square is undetermined.

### The canonical full word: finite marking and an exact coinvariant group

C.37 proves generation in every individual A5 quotient for the
canonical word of a GT0 involution. Its proof combines the complete
nineteen-orbit classification with full duality, binary lifting and
the one-section restriction argument. The full profinite restriction
theorem of Kochloukova and Zalesskii (2007, Theorem2) was reopened at
the required step. Separate generation does not prove generation
on the joint A5 head.

C.38 extracts a full-group consequence of that one-section argument.
For the auxiliary negative involution, the closed normal subgroup
generated by its twisted commutators is exactly the normal closure
of the third cusp. The coinvariant quotient is Zhat, and the cusp
is one actual commutator with a local reflection. This does not
identify the entire extension with a prescribed free product.

C.39 applies to every peripheral involution, without GT0 duality.
It proves S4 generation and an automorphism of each actual relative
pro-two completion over an S3 quotient. The finite Frattini argument
uses the two simple F2[S3] modules, leading only to the S4 case or
an abelianization contradiction; it never assumes descent to an
arbitrary finite quotient.

C.40 proves the correctly normalized third-cusp equation in every
S4 quotient for GT0 involutions. The multiplier is the previously
determined full dihedral unit d, with d=1 modulo four. The binary
GL2(F3) calculation couples the shared S3 values, actual negative
hexagon, full duality, R1 norm and proved generation conditions.
All six head choices have zero failures in the exhaustive necessary
finite tables. No table-realization theorem is invoked. The nine
distinct marked passports then give an automorphism on the actual
joint S4 finite head. The full d-third-cusp equation, its joint A5
analogue and universal conjugacy remain unresolved.

### The actual symplectic centralizer: a vanishing result and the complete surviving image

B.62 uses the genuine genus-five double cover branched at all twelve
punctures to define the projective symplectic representation. The
integral Burau comparison and odd-prime image were checked against
Gambaudo and Ghys (2005), Brendle et al. (2015), and Stylianakis
(2018, Theorem5.1). In the actual common marking, the two source
images modulo three have orders144 and1440 and intersection C3.
All seven integral relations in the tetrahedral homology kernel
kill that common image. The fixed zero-linking coordinate removes
the possible full-twist ambiguity. Hence the actual K has trivial
image in PSp10(F3). This is an actual nonnilpotent quotient result.

B.63 determines the next identity fibre modulo nine. Its source
dimensions are six and nine, with intersection dimension three.
Six actual words in the normal rigid kernel and nine cyclic
commutator words attain the exhaustive Schreier upper bounds;
they satisfy the integral homology conditions exactly. Three short
matched pairs span the whole intersection. The preimage M9 of
this group of order27 is a proper open, nonnormal subgroup of N3.
All two-factor refinements containing M9 have closed products.
The exact factor indices are3888 and1049760. Root independently
reconstructed the matrices, transversals, spans and literal words
in a different integral model.

B.64 determines the entire corresponding3-adic common image. The
actual integral A5 commuting order is the level-three Eichler order
with matrices [[a,b],[3c,d]]. Its natural ten-dimensional lattice
filtration is essential: the principal identity group has matrices
[[1+3a,3b],[9c,1+3d]] of determinant one. Ordinary two-by-two reduction
modulo three would give the wrong filtration. The six and nine
source directions span saturated Lie lattices of the connected
derived centralizers. Squared source words remove projective signs,
and a direct convergent logarithm and successive-cube argument
generates both derived principal groups. Their intersection in the
identity fibre is exactly the displayed three-dimensional A5 group.
The tetrahedral lifts remain in the closed rigid normal kernel and
both sources remain in their closed commutator subgroups.

This now proves compatible realizability at every3-power level in
the two source completions separately. It does not identify the two
lifts in the full mapping class completion. Thus further computations
in this same3-adic representation cannot remove that common image;
the remaining comparison is in its representation kernel or in an
actual joint quotient carrying additional tests. K is only known to
map into the common group, and neither K=1 nor K nontrivial follows.

## Seventh-checkpoint mathematical results and current limits

The recovered formal paper contains206 numbered statements:169 Proved,

requested task rows remain in the outcome table and coverage file. These
counts classify the actual scoped statements; they do not count solved
main questions. Both main questions remain Open.

**A.55: the exact elementary-two center.** We determine the actual scalar
center after adjoining an elementary-two residual layer over the specified
relative-pronilpotent base, under the stated trivial-action hypothesis.
The proof constructs the equivariant class-two extension, handles the
actual source generator squares by a C4 carry, and uses the reverse
commutator comparison to prove equality. It is stronger than an upper
bound from an exterior square. It makes no assertion about lifting an
involution or its literal peripheral frames.

**A.56: a forced self-dual finite layer with actual frame norms.** The
quotient Q6=S3×A4 supplies an actual odd residual head and a forced
nontrivial retained s-frame. Its36-dimensional F8 representation is
self-dual. The complete eight-dimensional cohomology space gives a
residual module of binary dimension864 and a central layer of binary
dimension108. The proof retains the original relative base, constructs
the actual group extension including the source carries, and solves
both the action and peripheral-frame equations. The remaining finite
problem reduces to277 binary variables, with a250-dimensional common
radical and a27-variable core. Re-execution found the same core
assignment27666, after18461 visits, and the same full witness
18677289404303020461252901246388292043973835622281379854.
The separate verifier evaluates the cusp, negative square, s-conjugacy,
s-frame square, r-conjugacy and r-frame square in the original group law;
all pass. Root independently reran that verifier. The all-translation
gauge argument retains every allowed old odd frame and the earlier
module family. We have not proved that these finite levels are cofinal.

**A.57: genuine nonsplit directions over the new base.** The coefficient
sequence from GR(4,3) gives an injective Bockstein on the actual new
base. Its kernel after inflation to the original source is exactly the
four-dimensional cusp summand. We construct the nonsplit pullbacks as
actual source quotients, and prove that four independent directions are
simultaneously attained. This identifies a concrete next obstruction
rather than assuming the old acyclicity repeats automatically. The
seventh-checkpoint theorem does not claim an involution on this new
quotient; that question is addressed in the separately retained
post-freeze integral analysis.

**B.65: the full cyclic rigid test.** The actual cyclic source also
centralizes the common dihedral group of order10. Of the twelve rigid
seven-point subsets, two give constant maps and the remaining ten form
one regular dihedral orbit. Their complete common kernel is therefore
the kernel of one explicit six-generator homomorphism into PB6. The
zero fixed-pair linking row proves the precise comparison with the
sphere quotient, including after completion. This is an exact reduction
of the full rigid constraint. Injectivity of that braid homomorphism,
and the corresponding statement for its embedded profinite image,
remain undecided.

**B.66: the common rational direction persists in every nilpotent class.**
The two actual source curves and their common marked base point give a
strictly pointed arithmetic diagram. An arithmetic element with an
infinite-order cyclotomic value acts by distinct scalars in the graded
degrees. Its finite-class spectral projectors produce compatible lifts
of the known common degree-two class. This proves a nonzero common
one-parameter subgroup in the rational prounipotent completions, fixed
by the icosahedral action and killed by the rigid tests. The proof does
not assert bounded integral denominators. Consequently it constructs
neither a nontrivial full profinite centralizer element nor a compact
pro-ell subgroup with the required common lift.

**C.41–C.42: finite detection of the canonical head.** We give an explicit
mod-two covering-chain model for complex conjugation, and then prove
the corresponding one-step detection result for involutions whose
sections are self-centralizing. The finite quotient F/Phi2(U) determines
the canonical word's image in F/U. The general proof uses the split
Hochschild–Serre spectral sequence and the verified full profinite
restriction theorem. It does not prove generation or the third-cusp
equation, and it does not discard a false-positive old head when a
nonzero correction remains in the new relation module.

## Eighth-hour results: exact centers, the integral tower, and the full braid lift

The formal additions are A.58–A.62, B.67–B.68 and C.43. The register now
contains214 statements:176 Proved,13 Refuted,19 Open and6 Known. The
bibliography has85 verified/unverified-labelled entries, and all49 requested
task rows remain. The main full involution-conjugacy decisions and the
icosahedral centralizer decision remain Open.

### Exact scalar centers and the actual Bockstein refinement

A.58 strengthens the scalar bound to an exact construction for every
finite abelian residual layer over the original relative-pronilpotent
base, and under explicit actual hypotheses over a more general base.
For each prime p the center is the O^p(H)-coinvariant exterior square of
the p-primary residual module. The proof controls both the Hom and Ext
terms, constructs the strict finite-head action, repairs the source
relations by cyclic carries, and compares the actual commutator map in
both directions. Thus it identifies the actual quotient, not just the
possible dimension of an extension class.

A.59 applies that theorem to the nonsplit Bockstein layer. The exact
center is C4^18 times C2^90, reducing to the earlier elementary center with
kernel of binary dimension18. This kernel is the actual first scalar
refinement, and the following commutator refinement is stable. The minus-
transpose relation retains diagonal2-torsion; omitting those terms or
counting the three Frobenius orbits twice would give the wrong center.

A.60 proves the actual negative action square using integral cusp
cohomology over GR(4,3). The free cusp module has rank4. The genuine
monomial coefficient involution is identity on its integral cusp
invariants and coinvariants, while its cohomological action has the
required minus sign. The source-cocycle formulas therefore give square
one as an equality of actual cocycle values. The kernel is independent
of the selected integral lifts. The central-character argument then
proves square one on the exact stable refinement as well.

A.61 handles the separate peripheral frame equations. Integral monomial
C2×C2 and S3 actions lift the prescribed affine frames. The exact group
law verifies s-conjugacy; r-conjugacy follows because a central2-primary
discrepancy between order-three elements has cube one. On the six free
center coordinates the negative action is identity, so its norm is
multiplication by2 and surjects onto the new center kernel. Independent
central halves kill both frame squares. The original C-frames and all
earlier families remain fixed, but the new A.56 central frame values may
change. The exact stored A.56 tuple is not asserted to extend. This is a
material scope distinction, confirmed by its separate restricted-family
compatibility calculation.

The final witness is stored in work_A/A61_integral_frame_solution.json.
Its independent verifier preserves that witness and checks both exact
conjugacies, both normalizer squares, the literal cusp, the action square,
and the prescribed finite heads in the original group law. Root and CE
reruns pass. The two pre-correction central squares have serializations
48596 and184959; both are killed by the recorded central halves. No
full negative square follows merely from this relative verification.

### A compatible tower at every two-power exponent

A.62 removes the exponent-four restriction. We choose the four cusp
cocycles once over the unramified cubic extension of Z2 and reduce them
at every exponent, retaining the four binary s-classes separately. This
produces actual nested source kernels and actual transition maps. The
residual abelianization at level n is L_n^4 plus V^4, and the exact
center is C_(2^n)^18 times C2^90. A single full positive lift supplies
compatible quotient actions whose negative squares are one.

The new step is an all-exponent s-centralizer lemma. The involution s
exchanges no opposite-weight pair: its conjugacy class in S3×A4 misses
the two possible transporters beta and beta*s. An s-invariant choice of
orientation removes the class-two carry integrally. A doubled s-fixed
free cusp vector then lifts to an actual s-centralizer at every n.
The finite orientation certificate checks36 weights,18 opposite pairs,
two fixed pair-orbits and eight two-element pair-orbits.

Both peripheral norm equations can consequently be solved at every
level. Central corrections may alter newer earlier-level frame values;
we do not assume that arbitrary individual frame choices lift. Instead,
a finite set of conditions is satisfied at its maximum exponent and
finite union of older families. Compactness gives a compatible family
of actions and literal involutory normalizers on the entire actual
inverse limit. Its kernel is relatively perfect, by stabilization of
all finite images of the descending kernels. The closed-normal lifting
criterion therefore applies directly to that inverse-limit quotient.
This is a specific class-two pro-two tower over the retained base; its
cofinality has not been established, and the full main question remains.

### The intermediate braid injection fails, and its full lift fills

B.67 refutes the proposed intermediate presentation
Q=Z²*Z²*Z for the five-strand image. The explicit word
w=[(ae)^(-1)f(ae),c] has a nonempty twelve-syllable free-product normal
form and a nontrivial image in S3, but its actual five-strand braid image
is the identity. The structural proof identifies an additional
stabilizer of the Birman fiber generator. The faithful Artin calculation
checks the complete word independently of that derivation.

The same word is nonidentity in the full six-strand target. B.68
classifies this surviving full lift. Its actual point-push word has
length144, cyclic length142. Six explicitly verified free-group
automorphisms reduce it to a24-letter cyclic word whose Whitehead graph
has10 vertices,15 edges, is connected, and remains connected after any
one vertex is deleted. The certificate verifies both inverse compositions
of each automorphism and records spanning trees for all11 connectivity
checks. Root independently reran the construction from the original
common-base eleven-strand braid words.

The published separable-set cut-vertex criterion, opened at Corollary3.5
of Heusener and Weidmann(2019), shows that the loop lies in no conjugate
proper free factor. On the six-punctured sphere, either side of an
essential separating curve has its fundamental group conjugate to a
proper free factor. The loop therefore fills. Kra(1981), Theorem2′ and
Theorem2(b), opened in the original article, identifies its point-push
with a pseudo-Anosov mapping class in the seven-punctured sphere.
This is a discrete classification of the surviving word. It proves
neither injectivity of the full map nor a completed-centralizer statement.

### Regular pants and canonical finite heads

C.43 proves that regular pants involutions have one section class and
self-centralizing sections. The proof retains the swapped boundary pair
through the full surface-pair duality and only then localizes. The two
remaining local endpoint restrictions have the same kernel after actual
coefficient transport, so the boundary rank calculation forces
surjectivity onto a single-section cohomology group. A finite permutation
module excludes a nontrivial centralizer. The result passes to the open
stable subgroups used in C.42, giving the actual first-dyadic head test.
It also makes the full common-normalizer transporter unique when it
exists. It does not recognize a free product or solve generation or the
third-cusp equation.

is replaced by the narrower results proved here.

### B.69–B.71: a full cyclic rigid relation and the exact joint obstruction to the test route

index, so Shusterman's primary-verified theorem makes it free profinite

### C.44: a full unit and an actual relative pronilpotent conjugacy

For a regular negative pants involution sigma normalized to invert z,
C.43 gives a unique solution g of sigma(g)g^-1=z. Let K be the closed
normal closure of z and A=F/K=Zhat. The compact Alexander module is
K/Phi_2(K)=F2[[A]][z], with the full procyclic head retained. The proof
computes the actual cyclic-cover homology transitions and proves that
every finite character of K extends to an open neighborhood; this
justifies the inverse limit comparison.

Multiplication by T-1 is an isomorphism onto the augmentation ideal.
Writing a for the image of g gives an exact equation theta D_a=1,
where (T-1)D_a=T^a-1. Augmentation forces a to be odd. Evaluation at
primitive roots of every odd prime order excludes divisibility by any
such prime. Thus a is a full profinite unit. The same Alexander module
calculation over every Fp and the pro-p Frattini property show that g,z
generate every relative pro-p quotient over A. The explicit product
comparison then gives generation of F/R_nil(K).

The substitution x to g and z to z is an automorphism of this relative
pronilpotent quotient, by finite generation and Hopficity, and its
generator equations conjugate the standard regular action to sigma.
The conjugacy is asserted in the automorphism group of the relative
quotient. It need not preserve the original three peripheral classes,
and the proof does not establish generation of full F. Additional
recognition-source readings are recorded with their precise hypotheses
and inaccessible steps in records/recognition_source_audit_09h.md;
none is used to replace those unresolved full conditions.

Only three distinct Frobenius families at one physical weight can
contribute a scalar triple. Their three cyclic tensors have one Jacobi

### The full dihedral centralizer

B.74 computes the full centralizer of J=<d,r>, isomorphic to D5.
For C=C_Gamma(C5), the commutator delta(c)=[c,r] lies in the central
C5 and is a homomorphism; its restriction to C5 is squaring. Hence
rho(c)=delta(c)^(-1/2)c is a canonical retraction, and

\[
C_\Gamma(C_5)=C_5\times C_\Gamma(D_5),\qquad
C_\Gamma(D_5)\simeq C_2*\mathbb Z.
\]

The parameter orbifold has two cusps and one cone point of order two.
Its actual degree-ten covering is q=(u^5+1)^2/u^5 from the ordered
cyclic parameter curve P1 minus{0,infinity,mu5}. The five points
u^5=-1 have local index two over the cone point0; the five deleted
points u^5=1 have index two over the cusp4; and0,infinity have
index five over infinity. Its deck group is D5, so the pure source
H5 is normal of index ten.

The valid prime-five comparison and the proved direct product give
the full profinite dihedral centralizer by taking the r-fixed factor.

permits permutation of the five non-pole ordinary labels. Its
intersection with Q8 is exactly H5. The canonical retraction has
infinite-index centralizer domain; it is not a finite-index ambient
retraction.

route. After fixing another ordinary label, it fills two extra cusps

The actual source E0=F semidirect complex conjugation has kernel
R=R_nil(F) with [R,E0]=R. On E0/R, complex conjugation on the two-primary
orientation factor and identity on every odd factor define a peripheral
automorphism fixing the external reflection, with mixed common
multiplier. It therefore already exists on its actual source-central
refinement. A full equivariant peripheral lift cannot exist: the full
fixed-group calculation forces literal powers of x and y, and A.39's
explicit S4 quotient refutes the third-cusp relation at multiplier7
modulo12. This is stronger than merely ruling out an involutory lift,

argument and its precise relation to A.18, A.34 and A.39 are saved in

suggested finite presentation refinement is explicitly unverified.

## Actual continuous abelianization

The exact closed filling kernel is Lbar/Nbar, where Lbar is the kernel
of the full free-factor projection Fhat11 to That3. The whole discrete
relation subgroup satisfies N contained in [L,L], hence Nbar is contained
in the closed commutator subgroup D of Lbar. The actual closed kernel's
continuous abelianization is therefore Lbar/D. The two explicit inverse
maps with Zhat[[That]]^8 semidirect That prove the compact module identity
directly. The coefficient ring is the inverse limit over finite
coefficient rings and finite quotient groups, not an unrestricted
product indexed by That. Delta identifies the two four-summand blocks
by its literal beta(t)x rule, giving the norm/difference exactness and
continuous Tate vanishing without interchanging cohomology and limits.

The mod-two color/level quotient detects that the invariant opposite-level
class remains outside the actual closed source image. This is a statement
about an abelian quotient, not a completed fixed-element lift.

One nonessential adjective in A's independent review needs correction:
its Section4 calls the two complementary coefficient summands clopen.
They are closed complementary direct factors; they are not open when
the other factor is infinite. The actual proof and the continuous
coordinate-swap isomorphism use only the closed direct-product
decomposition. The original review is preserved unchanged, and this
explicit correction records the exact scope of root's acceptance.

## An actual discrete nonlinear nonlifting theorem

Let A=X_(x,a) and B=X_(1,bar a) in the actual family kernel J. Projection
of the free Schreier group onto this pair kills the entire node relation
subgroup. Indeed the only four node pairs involving just these two
colors have level differences 0 or -1. Every signed source action
preserves these levels and colors, and retained-deck conjugation shifts
both levels equally. The selected pair has difference 1. Every defining
translated commutator therefore loses at least one core, irrespective
of its retained nonlinear conjugators. This is uniform over the entire
relation orbit.

The resulting actual retraction J to F(A,B) is delta-equivariant and
delta swaps A and B. Its discrete fixed subgroup is trivial by reduced
words. Hence the invariant abelian class A+B has no lift to J^delta.
This strengthens the earlier warning to a proved negative-lifting
statement about that specified class. It does not supply any fixed
element outside the actual source or refute the main fixed-source gate.

## Explicit next compact candidate; not yet asserted

Root proposed replacing the individual infinite Schreier-coordinate
projection by a finite-index construction. In the free F11 take
K3=ker(chi_x mod3), with its finite Schreier basis
x^r U_i x^-r, x^r f x^-r, x^r g x^-r for r=0,1,2, together with x^3.
Map the a-family at residue1 to A and its barred family at residue0 to B,
and every other basis generator to1. The selected residue difference1
is distinct modulo3 from the complete node differences0 and-1. The
candidate should kill every signed/deck node translate and descend to
the actual open subgroup W3. Its full completion would then provide
a continuous map on the actual closed J, avoiding any identification
with the abstract completion of discrete J. Delta sends the family
residue r to1-r and swaps the two colors, suggesting equivariance.

This candidate still requires a full marked Schreier proof, all-word
kernel argument, literal finite-basis equivariance and the completed
free-swap fixed-group theorem at use. It is the first CE continuation
task after immutable capture, not a checkpoint25 theorem.

## Twenty-sixth-hour research: an actual source kernel and nonlinear fixed lifting

# Twenty-sixth-hour research: an actual source kernel and nonlinear fixed lifting

Both main full decisions remain Open. The new results concern the actual
annular one-neck comparison in Main B, after its stated regional, cross
and prefix relations. They preserve the based source map, the whole
signed relation orbit and the actual induced profinite topology.

## The actual source map is noninjective

B.130 proves the exact identity

\[
U=s_1s_4^{-1}s_1^{-1}=a\tau a\tau,
\qquad [m,U]=1
\]

in the actual ordinary group. The proof retains the full raw contact
words. Three actual plus-regional node relations give four cross
commutations between the two subgroups generated by the relevant
mirror words and their selected-involution images. The norm map is
therefore multiplicative on that specified subgroup. A literal mirror
identity identifies the resulting source word with the norm of the
original meridian a. The actual inner generic and cross relations make
m commute with that norm.

The corresponding source word is freely and cyclically reduced of
length eight in the abstract free-six domain. Its image in the explicit
order-eight group UT3(F2) is nonidentity. Consequently the actual source
map is noninjective both discretely and on full profinite completions.
This settles the previously unproved source-injectivity possibility in
the negative. It does not supply a fixed element outside the source
image, and hence does not refute or prove the required fixed-source
equality or the main icosahedral centralizer statement.

B.124 proves that the five outer source generators still freely
generate an actual retract after all final relations. Its proof uses
the complete gap conjugator, the actual neck reflection and an explicit
inverse free basis. Together with the actual free-two base projection,
this gives exact abelianizations Z^6 and Zhat^6 for the discrete source
image and the actual compact source image. It does not identify the
abstract completion of the entire source image with its closure.

Those two actual detectors also give independent integral characters
on U and m. B.130 therefore proves that their commuting subgroup is an
actual Z^2 retract of both the ambient group and its source image. Its
closure is an embedded Zhat^2 continuous retract with the full induced
topology. Thus the new source relation has a concrete surviving
two-dimensional abelian subgroup, rather than merely an undetected
word in a quotient.

There is a further completed intermediate consequence. In the proper
intermediate amalgam of B.120 the corresponding commutator centralizes
the selected cone and is nontrivial even in the full completion. The
proof uses the completed corner intersection from B.126, the regional
full source topology, and compatible finite vertex quotients. In those
quotients the two factors remain outside the common edge, so their
commutator has reduced ordinary amalgam length four. Residual finiteness
of the resulting finite-vertex amalgam supplies a finite detector.
The final four-prefix filling kills this specified nontrivial element
of the completed intermediate centralizer. No embedding of an abstract
free profinite group of rank six in that intermediate centralizer is
assumed.

## Exact presentations and the final filling geometry

B.125 proves a proper full profinite three-vertex star for the original
ordinary-parity cover before the cross filling. Fully invariant finite
kernels give simultaneous exact edge kernels at both regional edges.
The two ordinary sheets of the cross relator have the same normal
closure. The actual quotient is therefore a single specified
commutator quotient of that star. The word occurring in the commutator
is an existing marked word; it is not introduced as an unconstrained
stable letter.

B.126 proves the exact corner face H intersect jHj equals the cyclic
prefix subgroup, both discretely and for the actual embedded compact
subgroups. On the ordinary Bass--Serre tree it also proves that the four
final prefix roots form a malnormal cyclic family. Distinct translated
axes have sharp overlap at most two edges, and every axis stabilizer is
the corresponding cyclic group. These ordinary axis results do not
give a strict small-cancellation bound for the length-four square
relators and are not transferred to the full profinite tree or the
filled quotient.

A separate complete research note proves why the particular proposed
HNN edge enlargement fails: a genuine domain commutation maps to a
reduced length-four commutator in the preceding proper amalgam. Thus
that proposed edge map is not a homomorphism. This is an exact failure
of the specified construction, not a claim excluding every alternative
graph-of-groups description.

## The whole family kernel and the actual compact module

B.127 gives the exact Schreier basis of the kernel of F11 onto the
retained free-three factor. It keeps every nonlinear connector of all
forty marked node words, every signed source operator and every
retained-deck translate. An exact composition rule supplies the
operators for arbitrary signed words. Every defining relator lies in
the commutator subgroup of that kernel, uniformly over the entire
normal closure. Its discrete abelianization is therefore the full
integral permutation module on the complete Schreier basis, with the
selected involution exchanging basis elements in pairs. Its Tate
cohomology vanishes in every degree.

B.128 constructs the continuous abelianization directly in the actual
closed family kernel. Two inverse continuous maps identify it with
eight copies of Zhat[[That]], where That is the retained free-three
completion. This uses the compact module universal property and
cofinal invariant open subgroups, without identifying the closure of
the infinite kernel with its abstract completion. The involution
again exchanges two closed complementary halves and gives explicit
norm and difference solvers, hence vanishing continuous Tate groups.

The exact signed source operators preserve each peripheral color and
the retained x-level. The four complete contact vectors occupy only
first-half level zero and second-half level one. A finite coefficient
and level-modulo-two quotient shows that the source has proper image
in the invariant module: the opposite-level class x e_a + e_bar_a is
outside that image. This abelian discrepancy by itself gives no fixed
element outside the source.

## Nonlinear nonlifting in the actual full completion

B.129 resolves the continuity question left in the previous checkpoint.
A finite-index residue-three construction gives an actual equivariant
free-two retract of the family kernel and a continuous retract of its
actual closed image. The construction factors through a free group of
rank twenty-four extended by a cyclic group of order three. Its
projection is applied only to the normal free fiber. Every translated
node has one core killed by the projection, for a uniform color and
level-difference reason; its arbitrary nonlinear conjugator is kept.

The involution swaps the two positive free generators of the target.
The target fixed subgroup is trivial, discretely and in full
completion. The profinite assertion uses the finite-factor centralizer
theorem of Herfort and Ribes (1985), with its exact hypotheses checked.
The invariant abelian class of xax^-1 times cac maps to (1,1), so it has
no fixed representative in either actual family kernel. This proves
that the paired module and its zero Tate groups cannot alone decide
the nonlinear fixed-source comparison.

## The exact one-sided image and strict source bounds

The literal contact basis is A_s=s1 s4^-1 s1^-1,

\[
P_1=(F(A_s,D_s)\times\mathbb Z_m)*F(B_s,E_s).

\langle\!\langle[m,A_s]\rangle\!\rangle
\ \subseteq\ \ker(E_5\to H_5)

\langle\!\langle[m,A_s],[m,D_s]\rangle\!\rangle.

## A stronger discrete conclusion: the actual pair generates F2

We keep the even-sum index-two subgroup of the retained free-three group,
of free rank five. In the index-two cover of F11, keep its entire retained

other fourteen family basis elements. The same complete odd-difference

\[
Q_7=F(B,\bar d)*T_2=F(B,D_0)*T_2,\qquad D_0=x\bar d x^{-1}.
\]

Both signed m operators descend. Their complete quotient diagrams are

The index-two source subgroup with even \(m\)-exponent has free basis \(m^2,u,mum^{-1}\).
